Rocket Report: Artemis II literally tore through the atmosphere; New Glenn delay

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Interstellar Technologies announces more government funding for the Japanese Zero rocket.

Welcome to Edition 9.18 of the Rocket Report! Blue Origin packed a lot of news into one afternoon Thursday with the reveal of a new kick stage for the New Glenn rocket, a new rocket engine design, and the minor and widely expected delay for New Glenn’s return-to-flight mission from December until January. The reconstruction of Blue Origin’s wrecked launch pad at Cape Canaveral, Florida, continues apace after a catastrophic explosion nearly six months ago. There’s a lot of pent-up demand from NASA, Amazon, and the Space Force for Blue Origin to get New Glenn back in action.

SpaceX vets work on water-fed propulsion. Three brothers who worked at SpaceX in the Falcon 9 era are now building satellites that run on water, a potentially harvestable resource on the Moon and found all over the Solar System, Ars reports. In late 2022, the three Carlisle brothers founded Argo Space to build infrastructure in space. Their vision was not just in-space transportation but also removing the need to use propellants from Earth to move around the heavens. Argo Space’s satellites will use water not only as a propellant, but also as a coolant and radiation shield.

How does this work? … Argo Space flew its first mission in July, but the spacecraft failed after one of its solar arrays didn’t deploy, and there wasn’t enough time to test the propulsion system. Two more missions are on Argo Space’s schedule for 2027 to demonstrate the company’s water-based thrusters. The propulsion system is basically a small rocket that uses hydrogen and oxygen as propellants. However, instead of launching with separate tanks of liquid hydrogen and liquid oxygen, the satellite carries a reservoir of liquid water. In space, the vehicle uses solar power to split water into hydrogen and oxygen gases and burns them to generate thrust.

JAXA backs small satellite launcher. Japanese launcher startup Interstellar Technologies has secured the backing of the Japan Aerospace Exploration Agency (JAXA) for its Zero rocket program, Aviation Week & Space Technology reports. The company said JAXA selected it as part of its Acceleration of Commercial Rocket Launch Demonstration program under the Space Strategic Fund. The government-funded program supports development and flight demonstrations of private launch companies with grants of up to $76 million per company, with the goal of fostering a growing commercial launch industry in Japan. Interstellar previously raised more than $250 million, with roughly equivalent funds from government and private capital.

Zeroing in … Interstellar is using the money to complete development of its Zero rocket, a two-stage launcher designed to carry up to 1 metric ton (2,200 pounds) of payload to low-Earth orbit. “We have achieved critical technical milestones, including Japan’s first successful firing test of an orbital-class methane engine,” said Satoshi Nakayama, director of the board and president of Interstellar. “We have now reached the stage where we must swiftly turn these proven development results into operational commercial services.” The most recent update from Interstellar indicated a first launch of Zero in 2027.

Launch demand outpacing supply. A new report from analytics firm Quilty Space confirms that, outside of China, the world faces a significant shortage of rockets over the next five years, Ars reports. The firm estimates that between now and 2030, there will be a shortfall of about 150 launches compared to demand from satellite operators and civil space agencies. If demand from the US military is added in—and SpaceX does not extend the Falcon 9 and Falcon Heavy program to accommodate increased national security needs—the shortage could be doubled.

Worse than we thought … “What we wanted to get at is to understand how severe this launch crisis is and when will it be resolved,” said Caleb Henry, director of research at Quilty Space. “What we learned is that the launch crisis is more severe than we thought and that it will not go away this decade for sure, and it might not abate until sometime late in the 2030s.” Why is this happening? Quilty puts the blame on several factors: the war in Ukraine taking Russian rockets off the Western market; Amazon Leo demand; developmental delays in a new generation of rockets, from New Glenn and Vulcan to Neutron, Terran R, and Nova; and more recently, the retirement of the Falcon 9 as a rocket for commercial service.

China continues launching mega-constellation. Another 18 satellites for China’s SpaceSail Internet network launched Tuesday (Monday, US time) from a spaceport in the country’s northwestern desert. The mission marked the second launch—and first operational flight—of the Kinetica 2 rocket developed by a Chinese startup known as CAS Space. The SpaceSail constellation, also known as Qianfan, now consists of some 275 flat-panel satellites from a series of launches that began in August 2024. The network’s first phase will number 1,296 satellites.

A mini-Falcon Heavy … The Kinetica 2 is an interesting launch vehicle, with three side-by-side boosters arranged in a manner similar to SpaceX’s Falcon Heavy. But the Kinetica 2 is a much smaller rocket, with three kerosene-fueled engines on each booster instead of nine. Its lift performance to low-Earth orbit is about 12 metric tons (26,000 pounds). CAS Space plans to eventually recover and reuse the Kinetica 2 boosters.

India launches strategic navigation satellite. A Geosynchronous Satellite Launch Vehicle (GSLV) rocket launched October 30 with a satellite to restore India’s regional navigation system, NDTV reports. The NVS-03 satellite is the third spacecraft in India’s second-generation navigation network. Some of the fleet’s older satellites suffered from atomic clock failures, rendering them partially or totally unusable. NVS-03 carries multiple clocks of different designs in an attempt to overcome the system’s reliability problems.

A “naughty boy” … India’s medium-lift GSLV has now launched 20 times since debuting in 2001. The rocket doesn’t fly as often as India’s smaller launcher, the PSLV, and hasn’t always performed well when it has. Six of the 20 flights ended in total or partial failures, but most of the mishaps occurred early in the rocket’s history. GSLV’s track record earned it the title “naughty boy” from Indian space officials. The most recent launch was a different story. The head of India’s space agency, V. Narayanan, declared the GSLV’s recent flight was the “most precise launch of any rocket in the world.” We’re not sure what metric Narayanan used to make that claim, but the launch was, indeed, a success.

China reveals roadmap to lunar landing. Chinese space officials confirmed that the Asian nation plans to land its astronauts on the Moon within three years, Ars reports. At the Seventh Manned Spaceflight Conference in Beijing, officials, including Wang Xiang, chief designer for the lunar exploration program, laid out the country’s most publicly detailed plans yet for a series of missions leading up to a crewed lunar landing “before 2030.” China’s lunar landings will involve a lunar-orbit rendezvous similar to what NASA did in the 1960s and early 1970s with the Apollo program. However, lacking a rocket as powerful as the Saturn V vehicle, the crew and lunar lander will launch separately to the Moon.

These are the rockets … China’s Long March 10 rocket family will be the workhorse for the country’s initial crew missions to the Moon. The next big test will be the demonstration flight of a Long March 10A rocket, which flies with a single core booster, and China’s new Mengzhou Moon ship to low-Earth orbit in early 2027. The larger Long March 10, with three boosters connected together, will launch China’s Lanyue lunar lander on an uncrewed test flight to the Moon in 2028. The next year, a follow-up mission will involve the concurrent launch of two Long March 10s, one with an uncrewed Mengzhou spacecraft and the other with a Lanyue lander that will dock together in lunar orbit. Then, before 2030, another dual launch of Long March 10s will occur, this time with crew on the Mengzhou spacecraft, leading to the crewed landing mission.

Blue Origin’s big reveals. Blue Origin announced two significant upgrades to its New Glenn rocket on Thursday, reducing the cost of manufacturing the vehicle and expanding its capacity for payload delivery to geostationary orbit and the Moon, Ars reports. With these upgrades, the company aims to eventually position New Glenn to launch more than 1,000 times a year. One of the announcements involved a new engine, called Northstar, that will eventually replace the BE-4 engine that powers the first stage of New Glenn. The other piece of news was that Blue Origin is developing a hydrogen-oxygen third stage for the rocket to give it an extra kick for high-energy missions.

Blue’s Northstar … The Northstar engine is a simplified but more powerful version of the BE-4 engine. It has half the number of parts and 30 percent less mass, and about 65 percent can be 3D-printed, all while providing a thrust increase over the BE-4. Blue Origin believes it can manufacture the engine at 25 percent the cost of a BE-4 engine. The kick stage is somewhat of an engineering marvel in itself. The stage will use a lightweight composite fuel tank, but officials were not sure the structure could contain leak-prone hydrogen at high pressure. The company recently had a breakthrough at Launch Site One in Texas, when it completed its first composite liquid hydrogen tanking test, giving engineers confidence to move forward with the design.

New Glenn won’t return to flight this year. Blue Origin now plans to resume launches of its New Glenn rocket in January, delaying the much-anticipated flight of the vehicle after a destructive launch pad explosion in May, Bloomberg reports. The Jeff Bezos-backed company had been driving to launch New Glenn again before the end of the year in its quest to challenge SpaceX. But Blue Origin Chief Executive Officer Dave Limp told employees on Wednesday that they are now targeting a flight early in 2027.

What’s a month? … The slight delay is unsurprising, and a January launch would still put Blue Origin well head of initial fears of how long it would take to rebuild its launch pad in Florida. New Glenn has a busy launch manifest after the return-to-flight mission, including a crucial launch in the spring of Blue Origin’s Blue Moon Mark 1 lander, an uncrewed precursor to the company’s future human-rated lander for NASA’s Artemis missions.

Artemis II tore through the atmosphere. The launch of NASA’s Artemis II mission in April caused significant disturbances in Earth’s ionosphere that could have triggered temporary, localized errors in GPS positioning, a new study has found, Space.com reports. The study by a team of researchers from National Cheng Kung University in Taiwan analyzed data from high-precision networks of fixed, ground-based GPS receivers. The researchers used the GPS measurements to reconstruct how the passage of the Artemis II mission’s Space Launch System rocket changed the concentrations of free electrons in the upper atmosphere.

Disturbing the peace … The measurements found that the ionospheric disturbances resulting from the Artemis II launch were more significant than any caused by other rocket launches to date. Curiously, they were up to 10 times more intense than those observed following the test launches of SpaceX’s Starship, which is twice as powerful as the SLS based on liftoff thrust.

November 6: Ceres 2 | Unknown Payload | Jiuquan Satellite Launch Center, China | 23:35 UTC

Ars Technica has been separating the signal from the noise for over 25 years. With our unique combination of technical savvy and wide-ranging interest in the technological arts and sciences, Ars is the trusted source in a sea of information. After all, you don’t need to know everything, only what’s important.

Sorensen urges Vancouver ‘to keep our competitiveness’ ahead of playoffs

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Jesper Sorensen has urged his Vancouver Whitecaps side to "keep our competitiveness" ahead of their Decision Day clash with rivals CF Montreal on Saturday.

Vancouver secured top spot in the Western Conference last time out with a 5-1 win over the Seattle Sounders.

The Whitecaps are in good form; after a run of four winless games (D3 L1), Vancouver have won three of their last four (L1) and scored nine goals in their last two games.

However, they have already lost to Montreal this season, losing 3-2 on aggregate in the Canadian Championship semi-finals after Montreal won the second leg 2-1 at home.

Sorensen wants a better result this time around, with the playoffs swiftly approaching.

"To try and win the game, of course, like I approach every time," Sorensen said when asked about his approach to the Montreal game.

"Obviously, we played there already once this year, and it didn't work out the best for us. Hopefully, we are going to be better this time.

"We will, of course, have one eye on the shorter term and one eye on the longer term [regarding team selection].

"It's also important that we keep our competitiveness and also the good performances we have had as of late, that we keep that going."

Full steam ahead to Decision Day  https://t.co/nxEF0e7hM5

— Vancouver Whitecaps FC (@WhitecapsFC) November 5, 2026

While Vancouver are top of the East, Montreal are rock bottom of the division, with just 26 points to their name.

They did manage to salvage a 1-1 draw with New York City FC on Saturday, eliminating the latter from playoff contention.

Prince Owusu enters the final match of the season with a team-high 21 direct goal contributions (15 goals, six assists) for Montreal.

Only Ignacio Piatti (three times) and Marco Di Vaio (in 2013) have more goal contributions in a single regular season for Montreal in the club's MLS history.

Brian White scored twice in Vancouver's win over Seattle on Sunday to take him to 20 goals in the 2026 regular season.

White needs two goals on the final day of the season to equal the Whitecaps' single-season scoring record of 22, set by Camilo Sanvezzo in 2013.

MATCH PREDICTION: VANCOUVER WHITECAPS WIN

Vancouver enter the final match of the season on 61 points, having already secured the top seed in the West. A win would give the club a new single-season points record, surpassing the 63 they collected last year.

Montreal have won only two of their last 22 regular-season matches dating back to mid-May (D8 L12), including collecting just two points from their last six (D2 L4). No team has collected fewer points than Montreal's 14 since that run began on May 13.

However, Montreal are unbeaten in seven straight at home against the Whitecaps in all competitions, last losing at home in April 2017.

De la Fuente includes Como’s Ramon among three newcomers for Nations League

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Luis de la Fuente has named three new faces in Spain's squad for their final two Nations League games.

Spain have already secured their place in the quarter-finals ahead of their meetings with Czechia and England this international break.

And De la Fuente has used it as an opportunity to make seven changes to the squad that was named in the extended break earlier in the season.

Real Betis pair Alvaro Valles and Marc Roca are among the new faces, along with Como's Jacobo Ramon.

Ramon has caught the eye for Como, who have made an impressive start to the 2026-27 season, including a historic 9-1 win in the Champions League against Lens on Wednesday, with the defender getting an assist.

Joan Garcia and Pedro Porro both missed the last camp through injury but have recovered and been welcomed back into the fold.

U.S. economy added 165,000 jobs in October, way more than expected; unemployment rate slips to 4.1%

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The U.S. economy added jobs at a much stronger than expected pace in October, while the unemployment rate moved lower as the labor market showed surprising strength.

Nonfarm payrolls increased by a seasonally adjusted 165,000 for the month, compared with the Dow Jones estimate for 59,000, the Bureau of Labor Statistics reported Friday.

The unemployment rate nudged down to 4.1%, compared with the forecast for 4.2%. A more encompassing measure that includes discouraged workers and those holding part-time jobs for economic reasons rose to 7.9%, up 0.3 percentage point, amid an increase of 421,000 in the civilian labor force.

In addition to the strong payroll gains for October, prior months were revised up substantially: The August figure moved up to 186,000, an upward revision of 53,000, while the September count moved to 60,000 following an upward revision of 31,000.

Payroll gains also came in better than forecast. Average hourly earnings increased 0.4% on the month and 3.1% annually, compared to respective estimates for 0.3% and 2.9%. The average work week was unchanged at 34.4 hours.

The breadth of job growth also was strong, contrary to a trend in which health care had accounted for the bulk of gains.

Professional and business services saw an increase of 70,000 to lead all sectors, while health care was next at 42,000. Other areas seeing notable growth included transportation and warehousing (25,000) and manufacturing (19,000).

The report comes the same week as an election in which the economy and in particular inflation was the center of attention. Democrats staged a nationwide rout that will give the party control of both houses of Congress after campaigning heavily on addressing affordability issues.

For the Federal Reserve, the report also comes at a pivotal time. Central bankers are weighing whether to resume raising interest rates in December as inflation remains stubbornly above the central bank's 2% target.

Traders increased the probability of another quarter percentage point hike when the Federal Open Market Committee meets Dec. 8-9. Odds rose to 76.8%, according to the CME Group's FedWatch tool, though the indicator pointed to only one more hike after. The odds of further hikes in 2027 have fallen somewhat in recent days, after the midterm election rout and Thursday's sell-off in tech stocks.

Market reaction otherwise was muted, with a mixed picture on stock market futures heading into the open. Treasury yields were higher across the board.

Mandatory Nier: Automata Update Accidentally Requires 366GB: ‘I Just Need To Delete 90 Percent Of My Computer To Install This’

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Some people who tried to play Nier: Automata this week on Steam ran into an unusual problem. The game required a new update and it was asking to take over people’s hard drives. A game that usually had an installation footprint of less than 50GB ballooned to 366GB. It’s still unclear why.

“Thanks to the latest update, fresh install of the game is now whooping 366GB,” read one angry review on Steam from someone with over 100 hours played of the sci-fi action-RPG. “Why? Because each DLC from YoRHa Edition is now the same size as the main game is. EVERY. SINGLE. ONE.”

Another, responding to the idea that the massive size was because the core install now included every one of the game’s optional language packs, wrote, “Like, can you leave perfectly functional games alone and add a ‘Language pack DLC’ ??? Repair your mistakes.” A third was more succinct: “366gb for 2b’s big butt I guess.”

A 1.9-Million-Year-Old Secret in the Jordan Valley Could Rewrite the Story of Human Migration

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

New dating puts ‘Ubeidiya alongside the oldest known Eurasian settlements—and hints that early humans arrived in waves.

In a sun-baked stretch of the Jordan Valley, not far from the Sea of Galilee, the ‘Ubeidiya archaeological site has yielded hominin fossils long thought to belong to migrating populations that crossed what is now Israel between 1.2 and 1.6 million years ago. Their remains and tools were intermingled with the bones of other animals, many of which have long since gone extinct. Although the site was already ancient, it gave up one more secret when its geological age was re-analyzed using its rock layers. ‘Ubeidiya has now been found to be 1.9 million years old.

Long before it dried up, this primeval Levantine paradise was a freshwater lake. A stream that flowed into it left behind sediment deposits containing hominin and animal fossils. Also found at ‘Ubeidiya were tools made by hominins who lived on the lake’s shores, crafted from flint, basalt, and limestone from the surrounding area. The sediment originally settled in even, horizontal layers, but these were tilted when the center of the Jordan rift subsided. A kibbutz member accidentally discovered the site in 1959 while plowing, and excavations began the following year, with heavy machinery needed to expose its rock strata. Not until 2021, however, did archaeologists Omry Barzilai and Miriam Belmaker begin to suspect that hominin populations might have occupied the site earlier than previously thought.

Their re-analysis showed that the sediments had a more complicated history than once assumed. “The exposure-burial history that emerges from the model implies recycling of sediments previously deposited and buried in the rift valley…subsequently exhumed, eroded, transported and then redeposited along the ‘Ubeidiya paleo lake shoreline,” they said in a study recently published in Quaternary Science Reviews.

Together with archaeologist Ari Matmon of the Hebrew University of Jerusalem, Barzilai, who teaches at the University of Haifa, and Belmaker, of the University of Tulsa, led a team of researchers in dating sediment samples. They used three advanced methods that proved effective on material that’s long been difficult to date. First, cosmogenic isotope burial dating established how long the rocks had been buried. This method measures the decay of rare isotopes produced when cosmic rays interact with rocks at the surface. Second, fossilized shells from freshwater snails of the genus Melanopsis were found in the same layer as the hominin-made stone tools. Uranium-lead dating, which measures the decay of uranium into lead, gave a minimum age for those tools.

The third line of evidence came from ancient records of Earth’s magnetic field orientation, which were captured in lake sediments that later turned to rock. The researchers compared these records with the documented changes in the magnetic field’s polarity over time, known as geomagnetic reversals. The comparison showed that the lake’s sediment deposits had formed during the Matuyama Chron. Chrons are intervals of time defined by their magnetic polarity, and the Matuyama, a period of reversed polarity, began at the dawn of the Quaternary. Together, the three methods pushed back the date of hominin habitation to 1.9 million years ago. This is about the same age as the Dmanisi site in Georgia, where Homo erectus settled before spreading through Eurasia. Dmanisi had long been regarded as one of the oldest known settlements outside Africa.

Matmon, Belmaker, and Barzilai have suggested that this age means ‘Ubeidiya may once have been home to both Oldowan and Acheulean cultures. The oldest stone tools made by hominin hands come from Oldowan sites in Africa that are up to 2.5 million years old. (Some more debated sites in Ethiopia and Kenya go back 3.4 million years). By the time hominins reached ‘Ubeidiya, the flaking and fracturing involved in stone toolmaking had advanced. Acheulean hominins refined it further and also standardized the process. Their tools were strong enough to be used as weapons or for butchering animals for meat. The researchers think Oldowan and Acheulean hominins may have coexisted in the region. They also think Acheulean technological advances in the Levant occurred earlier than assumed.

“The revised age has significant implications for understanding the routes and timing of early human dispersal,” they said. “[This prompts] a reassessment of early Homo biogeography, dispersal pathways, and the possibility of multiple or overlapping migration events out of Africa.”

Elizabeth Rayne is a creature who writes. Her work has appeared in Popular Mechanics, Ars Technica, SYFY WIRE, Space.com, Live Science, Den of Geek, Forbidden Futures and Collective Tales. She lurks right outside New York City with her parrot, Lestat. When not writing, she can be found drawing, playing the piano or shapeshifting.

A Guy’s Guide to the Many (Many) Dick Myths of 2026

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

A urologist right-sizes the manhood myths that have recently sprung up online.

Our product picks are editor-tested, expert-approved. We may earn a commission through links on our site. Why Trust Us?

From biohacker Bryan Johnson to looksmaxxer Clavicular, every influencer claims to have the secret to looking bigger, getting harder, and lasting longer. “Penis-maxxing can run the gamut,” says Rena Malik, MD, a urologist, sexual health expert, and Men's Health advisor. “It's a huge market.” (Pun probably intended.) Do any of their tactics work though? The jury is out, but the doctor is in: “I want people to think about the long-term health of their penis, not just right now,” Dr. Malik says, which is why she wrote a whole book about it (The Hard Truth, out now). Here, she right-sizes the manhood myths that have recently sprung up online.

Sometimes (note: not “often”), the ingredients in these supplements do work for some men (note: not “all”—or “many”). That could be because of L-arginine and L-citrulline, ingredients that increase nitric oxide and allow more blood to flow to the penis, says Dr. Malik. Or it could also be the placebo effect.

The supplement industry is largely unregulated and many products have little to no scientific backing. Some supplements even contain contraband PDE5 inhibitors, like tadalafil and sildenafil, or animal testosterone. Which means they might work, but they can also interact with other meds or come with side effects, Dr. Malik says. Plus, the time you waste trying different OTC options can lead to (gulp) shrinkage as erectile tissue becomes fibrotic. So, if you think you have ED, skip the supplements and see your doc, like, now.

The theory behind jelqing, which is giving yourself an extra intense hand job, is that doing so creates micro-tears. In muscle-building, at least, the healing of micro-tears can result in strength and size overall. But…

…your penis isn’t a muscle. Which means that jelqing could cause serious irreversible damage to the blood vessels in your penis and to your penis itself. The so called “technique” has no science behind it and can even cause permanent ED, Dr. Malik says. You’re better off working out your pelvic floor.

Some research does link ejaculation to certain health improvements (less stress, deeper sleep, even immune function).

A lot of guys think they need to have sex or masturbate whenever they get hard to keep their erectile tissue healthy. “You need to keep getting erections to keep your tissue healthy, and your body does that as long as you're healthy,” says Dr. Malik. If you regularly notice that you’re not hard when you wake up in the middle of the night, or you don’t have morning wood, see your doc. It may be a sign of fibrosis of the erectile tissue, which can lead to penis shrinkage.

In specialized cases, a penis pump or medical-grade vacuum erection device (VED) can help some men. Both devices create negative pressure to draw blood into the penis, which can result in an erection. For guys about to get a penile implant, a penis pump can help them “get a little bit of a larger implant,” says Dr. Malik. For men who have had a radical prostatectomy (that’s a surgery for prostate cancer that can lead to a loss of penile length), they can maintain their penile length with VED.

There's little to no data that penis pumps work to enhance length or girth for the average guy, Dr. Malik says. In fact, the European Association of Urology reviewed all the science on penile enhancement with VEDs and found scarce evidence that these devices provide any aesthetic benefit. What does: Manscaping. An overgrown bush can hide a large part of your penis, so grooming your pubic hair is easy (and way cheaper).

Testosterone levels do start to dip after age 40. And T factors into a strong libido, erectile function, and sexual satisfaction. For men who develop testosterone deficiency and experience symptoms (low desire, ED, etc.), TRT can help.

Testosterone levels fluctuate all the time—"the variability even within individuals ranges from 18 to 50 percent,” says Dr. Malik. So, if you haven’t been into sex, or can’t get it up once in a while, your T may not be the issue. First focus on eating healthier, reducing stress, and sleeping better. If the issues stick around, then see a doctor.

Honestly, there isn’t one. Your penis is fine.

Being bigger won’t necessarily make you better in bed. That’s because 85 percent of women need clitoral stimulation to orgasm, Dr. Malik says, which typically doesn’t happen through vaginal penetration alone. Because the clitoris is not located at the back of the vaginal canal (that’s the cervix, and hitting it can be painful for some women), a longer penis doesn’t mean more pleasure. A skilled tongue, faster fingers, and/or the right tools (read: a vibrator) will though.

Lindsay Geller is the Lifestyle Director at Women’s Health, where she oversees the Life, Sex & Love, and Relationships sections on WomensHealthMag.com and the Mind section of Women's Health magazine. When she's not writing or editing articles about the latest dating trends and pop culture phenomenons, she's usually watching reality TV or playing with her dog, Lucille (Go Fetch That) Ball.

Here's How Many Shares of This Unstoppable Dividend Stock You'd Need for $10,000 in Yearly Passive Income

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

It's beating the market by more than triple this year.

Dividend stocks are an important component of a well-diversified portfolio at any time, and Coca-Cola (KO -0.07%) is one of the best. It's a Dividend King, which means it has raised its dividend for at least 50 years, and it has one of the best track records on the list, with 64 consecutive increases. That's reliably growing passive income.

But getting $10,000 annually in dividends from Coca-Cola stock may require a bigger investment than you realize.

Coca-Cola is crushing the market this year, up 38% versus the S&P 500's (^GSPC -4.36%) 12% gain. It's been performing phenomenally despite inflation, and investors see the stock, which has been a longtime favorite of investing legend Warren Buffett, as a safe haven against artificial intelligence (AI) stocks.

However, the high stock price means the dividend yield is lower than usual, because price and yield move inversely. Today, Coca-Cola's dividend yield is 2.2%.

It pays $2.12 in annual dividends per share as of its February increase, so you'll need 4,717 shares to reach $10,000 in annual dividends. At the current price, 4,717 shares would cost $452,926.

That's nearly half of $1 million in Coca-Cola stock alone. Unless you have much more than that to invest in a variety of dividend stocks, it makes more sense to spread your money around and benefit from other dividend stocks instead of concentrating too much in one stock.

These 8+ PS5 Games Will Be Delisted in November and December 2026

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The end of the year is always a prime time for PS5 game delistings and server closures, but fortunately we’re being spared a large list this year.

There are about eight titles with impending changes you need to be aware of, so here’s the full list:

Sony game Destruction AllStars will go completely offline on 25th November, following some alterations earlier in the year.

This will render most of the single player content unplayable, outside of a few arcade challenges, although the platform holder says your overall experience may be affected in those too.

In-game purchases will then be removed from Synduality: Echoes of Ada, the Bandai Namco live service shooter. This is the beginning of the publisher repurposing the game as a single player-only experience, with more changes planned in 2027.

PSVR2 MMO Zenith: Nexus will go offline on 18th December, and then the end of the year brings a slew of delistings, primarily from publisher GameMill.

This is due to licenses expiring for franchises like Avatar: The Last Airbender and Street Outlaws. If you purchase these games before they’re removed, they’ll remain in your library.

While it never officially released, PlayStation fans may want to pay attention to the impending closure of Ratchet & Clank: Ranger Rumble, which will be removed from mobile storefronts on 21st November.

One final thing to note is that 2K Sports will disable the online servers of NBA 2K25 on 31st December, but single player modes will remain.

That’s the full breakdown of everything being delisted in November and December 2026, then. I’ll update should any other announcements be made before the end of the year.

This Low-Profile Roborock Vacuum and Mop Is $500 Off Ahead of Black Friday

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Pradershika Sharma is a tech deals writer for Lifehacker. She has a Master’s degree in English Literature, a B.Ed., and a TESOL certification. She has been writing professionally since 2018, creating product reviews, affiliate articles, and search ads for global clients while working with Rubix Agency and Cognizant. Previously, she spent a year teaching English at the junior high level.An avid reader since childhood, Pradershika's idea of extreme sports is staying up to read “just one more chapter.” She lives in India.

The Roborock Qrevo Edge 2 robot vacuum and mop is down to $799.99 from $1,299.99 in an early Black Friday deal, saving you $500 and bringing it to its lowest price to date, according to price trackers. Much of its appeal comes from a slim 3.1-inch profile that lets it reach under beds, sofas, and other low furniture where taller robot vacuums often can’t fit. It manages this with a feature Roborock calls RetractSense, which checks the clearance as the robot approaches a tight gap and lowers the laser navigation tower into the body, leaving a camera with a 100-degree field of view to guide it underneath.

Beyond its low profile, the Qrevo Edge 2 pairs 25,000Pa of suction with a split DuoDivide main brush that guides hair toward the suction opening, so long strands and pet fur are less likely to end up wound around the roller. Edges get extra attention too, since its FlexiArm Arc side brush reaches out into corners while its mop module swivels to get closer to walls, with the two mop pads spinning at 200 RPM as they work. It also adjusts its cleaning based on whether it encounters dry or wet dirt, boosting suction for dust and lifting its main brush over spills. For all that adaptability on the floor, getting between rooms can be trickier—the robot lacks an adjustable chassis to lift itself over obstacles, so it can struggle with thresholds higher than 0.8 inches.

Once a cleaning run wraps up, most of the upkeep happens at the dock, which washes and dries the mop pads and empties dust into a bag rated for up to 65 days. That said, its onboard water tank is relatively small at 2.3 fluid ounces, potentially requiring extra trips to the dock during larger mopping jobs. There's also no dedicated cleaning solution tank, meaning you'll have to add approved cleaning additives manually to the fresh water. The rest of the fine-tuning happens in the Roborock app, where you can set cleaning schedules, adjust suction power and water flow, and choose how the robot handles carpets.

In short, yes, Black Friday still offers discounts that can be rare throughout the rest of the year. If there’s something you want to buy, or you’re shopping for gifts, it’s a good time to look for discounts on what you need, especially tech sales, home improvement supplies, and fitness tech. Of course, if you need to save money, the best way to save is not to buy anything.

Black Friday sales officially begin Friday, Nov. 27, 2026, and run throughout “Cyber Week,” the five-day period that runs from Thanksgiving through Cyber Monday,  November 30, 2026. But Black Friday and Cyber Monday dates have expanded as retailers compete for customers. You can get the same Black Friday sales early, and we expect sales to wind down by Dec. 2, 2026.

Yes, Amazon has Black Friday sales, but prices aren’t always what they seem. Use a price tracker to make sure you’re getting the best deal, or refer to guides like our live blog that use price trackers for you. And if you have an Amazon Prime membership, make the most of it.

“The Biggest Role of My Life — So Far”: Breakout Star Reggie Absolom on Putting the ‘Kid’ in ‘Club Kid’

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The 14-year-old Brit discusses owing his music taste to onscreen dad Jordan Firstman, shooting with queer performers and why a failed 'Harry Potter' audition was the best thing that ever happened to him.

While in the running for the titular role in HBO’s upcoming Harry Potter adaptation, British star Reggie Absolom made an impression.

The young actor had caught the eye of prolific U.K. casting director Lucy Bevan, who had been assigned the rather enormous job of sifting through tens of thousands of kids gunning to play Potter, Ron Weasley and Hermione Granger in the hotly anticipated reboot.

Bevan was able to ease the pain of rejection for Absolom by delivering news of another movie she was working on, a comedy-drama from a buzzy U.S. writer-director named Jordan Firstman. “Jordan needed a U.K. casting director for the U.K. child because it was an important role in the film,” Absolom begins to The Hollywood Reporter about Bevan’s search for his character, Arlo. “[Firstman] needed someone who’s done it before, who knows kids that could be put up for this role. And he definitely went to the right casting director,” he smiles. “She’d seen me for other things before, but it was mainly Harry Potter that made me get put forward for that.” Related Stories Movies For 'Father's House,' Former Cult Members and Lauded Actors Collaborated to Explore Mechanisms of Control in a Cinematic Experiment Movies "Saviors of Cinema" and "Money Machine": Tallinn Puts Spotlight on Kids, Youth Films and Social Media Bans

At 14 years old, Absolom is already the picture of professionalism. We’ve sat down during October’s chaotic BFI London Film Festival to talk about the smash-hit success of Club Kid, Firstman’s debut feature that gave the Cannes Film Festival a much-needed punch to the gut with its combination of laugh-out-loud humor and tear-jerking warmth. Out in U.S. and U.K. theaters Nov. 6, the movie follows Firstman’s washed-up, queer party promoter in New York, Peter, who gets the shock of his life when 10-year-old Arlo shows up saying he’s Peter’s biological son. Cue a frantic attempt to get his act together (and one glorious scene where Absolom’s character steals an “I Love Björk and Bareback” T-shirt from his dad’s wardrobe).

You’d be hard-pressed to find a Cannes premiere as high-spirited as Club Kid‘s. Absolom stood on stage with his onscreen father and co-star Cara Delevigne, and afterwards was lofted into the air by the duo as a misty-eyed audience showered the group with applause. The good vibes carried on with the year’s most competitive bidding war, eventually won by A24. All of a sudden, this teenage actor from just outside Oxford, who’s grown up on Despicable Me and Swashbuckle, found himself as close to Hollywood acclaim as anyone. Harry Potter who?

Here, in this room at Claridge’s Hotel on a dry and sunny day in London, he’s still pinching himself. “I was so excited to go there, and then when I got there, I thought, ‘What if people don’t like it?'” Absolom reflects on his trip to the world’s glitziest film festival. “I got a bit nervous, but when I watched it, people were laughing. And the standing ovation at the end, if you rewatch it, you can see I’m trying to hold back tears the whole time.”

Every critic was in agreement: Part of Club Kid‘s undeniable charm is the maturity and authenticity with which Absolom embodies Arlo. A lost child whose dysfunctional upbringing makes his doe-eyed attachment to Peter even more painful, the role lived and died on the chemistry between Absolom and Firstman. It’s no surprise, then, that Firstman spent his birthday a couple of years ago walking around a London park with the child, his mother, and an ice cream cone. “We had such a good, flowing conversation,” Absolom remembers. “It wasn’t small talk [or] icebreaker stuff. It just felt like talking to someone I had known for years already.”

The two immediately bonded over their love of music, and Firstman actually made a Spotify playlist for the kids auditioning for the role. “I took some photos of the songs that I liked because I wanted to get a gauge. You can’t play Arlo if you haven’t got good music taste,” says Absolom, referencing his character’s DJ aspirations. Two bands in particular have left a lasting impression, tying him to Firstman forever: Massive Attack and the Cocteau Twins. “I’m still thankful for that because I love the Cocteau Twins. They’re my favorite band,” he beams.

With this crucial bit of prep in his arsenal, Absolom, backdropped by his bathroom — “That’s where we do all our self-tapes; it’s the best place in our house” — got on a Zoom call with Firstman, Bevan and producer Alex Coco. He’d landed the job. “I knew I had a chance because everyone does,” he explains, “but I never really expected to get it, and it was such a shock. I was genuinely speechless. Just staring at the screen [with] a blank face, and then: ‘Oh my god, I’ve just landed the biggest role of my life so far.'”

It must be said that the shoot sounds like any teenager’s dream: Six weeks in New York with two days off a week, where Absolom went with his family to all of the obligatory tourist stops, like the Flat Iron and Empire State Buildings and the Statue of Liberty. The only sizeable set he’d been on until this point was the BBC and BritBox’s The Other Bennet Sister, in which he plays a small role as son to the Gardiners. But Club Kid, even as an indie, felt much larger.

For a kid whose only other credit was a John Lewis Home Insurance ad, the difference was initially slightly jarring. “There was more people,” Absolom concedes, “but I just felt like I knew all of them.” Being thrust into a room with real-life drag queens, trans people and queer performers was as vibrant, inviting, and as deliciously fun as you’d expect. “They were all so nice to me,” he continues. “And Jordan, even though he had so much on his plate — he had to think about directing, did a bit of producing, and obviously having to remember his lines and all — made me feel so welcome whilst doing all of that. He also helped me out acting-wise by teaching me that you can improv and you can suggest to change a line or do something a different way. You don’t have to be scared to talk to the director like that.”

In fact, one of the film’s best scenes is injected with a shot of real sentiment. “After I come out of the club for the first time, and we’re in the car on the way back to [Peter’s] apartment, and we’re celebrating because everyone was so happy about it.” There weren’t any set lines — Firstman had told Absolom to be amazed: “‘It’s the best day of your life ever,'” he remembers his director saying. This unsuspecting tidbit makes the onscreen conversation that unfolds between them even more tenderhearted.

We talk about Arlo’s future — a sequel, Absolom jokes, where he’s pursuing a career on the DJ decks stateside. When asked if there was any part of the shoot that he may have found difficult, he sheepishly tells THR: “Probably the most challenging bit was having no other kids on set, and also no other British people.” (He did, thankfully, have the opportunity to teach his driver some British insults — like calling someone a “mug” or a “melt.” Love Island U.K. viewers, this is your time to shine.)

And Absolom has so far managed to balance the jet-setting life of a celebrated actor with, well, being a kid. “Film festivals are normally on the weekends anyway, so I really only miss two days of the week,” he says. When I suggest that a statement like that must have his friends thoroughly envious, he replies: “I have two friend groups. […] And I think the ones I have out of school are more interested in it. When it comes out in the U.K., they’ll be old enough to watch it. I won’t!” (The film is rated 15 here in Britain, so you’ve got to be 15 or over to watch — Absolom’s first slice of industry irony.)

He finds himself in some pretty extraordinary company, with British child stars like Owen Cooper of Adolescence fame and 28 Years Later‘s Alfie Williams among his peers. But another sharp display of wisdom comes in Absolom’s pragmatism about what the next few years might hold. “I’m at a trickier age,” he tells THR. “The voice can go, and you don’t want to be halfway through filming and then the voice goes. So it’s gonna be, maybe, a gray area for a year or two.”

After that? Anything is possible if Hollywood’s big, sweaty embrace of Club Kid is anything to go by. Absolom’s dream role would be working with Christopher Nolan — he had his mind blown by Interstellar earlier this year — or Ryan Gosling, whom he loved watching in Project Hail Mary. He is, in part, still processing the last wild few months, which peaked when his mother came into his room recently and showed Absolom’s name in the 30th spot on Variety‘s early projection for the top 30 supporting actors at the Oscars. “A nomination is out of the picture right now,” says Absolom — that celebrity humility creeping in already — “but just being on the list is mental. I’d be happy with a nomination for anything. Winning is just a crazy, extra bonus.”

Manchester United: supporters applauded Carrick and booed their players

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

The atmosphere is becoming increasingly heavy at Manchester United. After the heavy defeat conceded at Old Trafford against Bournemouth (0-3), Red Devils supporters sent a clear message to their team. At the final whistle, Michael Carrick was applauded by part of the public, while his future on the Mancunian bench appears more and more uncertain.

But the support shown for the coach was not extended to his players. As soon as Carrick disappeared into the tunnel leading to the changing rooms, the applause gave way to whistles aimed at the Mancunians. A striking contrast which illustrates the growing anger of supporters, while Manchester United are in a worrying 16th place in the Premier League, before an already crucial meeting against Chelsea.

The Best Women’s Base Layers of 2027

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

If you buy through our links, we may earn an affiliate commission. This supports our mission to get more people active and outside.Learn about Outside Online's affiliate link policy

The best women's base layers of 2027 are breathable, durable, and warm. (Photo: Rick Sorensen)

What to know: The best base layers manage moisture and regulate body temperature in cold weather. After testing all last winter, our team of expert testers named the Smartwool Thermal Merino Mock Neck the best overall base layer for women, and the Kari Traa Saga Half Zip and Pants the warmest base layer for women. Keep reading for four more top recommendations.

You can’t construct a strong building on a weak foundation. The same goes for layering. The best waterproof shell in the world won’t save you if you don’t wear a solid base layer underneath. But instead of concrete and lumber, base layers are built from fabric—technical when you’re skiing, and cozy for après or yurt life. The market is flooded with options these days, from high-quality natural fibers and proprietary blends to fun patterns and various fits.

Our crew of badass mountain women put almost 70 base layer tops and bottoms through the wringer last winter on hut trips in British Columbia, resort skiing in the Swiss Alps, and backyard missions in the Tetons, Sierra Nevada, and Cascade Mountains. We braved blisteringly cold temps, relentless storms, slushy spring conditions, and even a little bit of rain, evaluating the best women’s base layers for warmth, comfort, breathability, style, and fit.

Pros and Cons + Stylish for après and town wear + Midweight design is highly versatile – Mock neck can interfere with hoods

We hardly went any days last winter without the Smartwool Classic Thermal Merino Mock Neck base layer. Comfortable and cute, this long-sleeve joined us for bell-to-bell days at Jackson Hole and hut trips in Switzerland. “I loved that I could wear it all day and into the evening too,” said one tester after skiing in Italy. “It’s like a cute winter top that’s technical. Plus, it never stinks.”

The full merino wool construction provided optimal warmth and breathability on the hill. Testers wore this layer under shells and insulation and even on its own in temps ranging from −10 to 52 degrees F, dubbing it “the most versatile layer in the closet.”

The fit isn’t as snug as other base layers for women—so it’s flattering and relaxed enough to pair with jeans but fitted enough to keep chilly air from sneaking in. The only complaint? A couple testers felt the mock neck was bulky when paired with a neck gaiter and a stack of hoods.

Pros and Cons + High value + Comfortable fit – Top isn’t super warm

Merino wool for under $50? You have our attention. Quince usually isn’t our first stop for outdoor gear, but when we saw affordable merino base layers for women among their offerings, we knew we needed to take a set into the mountains. After wearing them on storm days and springlike conditions at Jackson Hole Mountain Resort in February, testers were impressed with Quince’s basic and lightweight merino wool long-sleeve and leggings.

“If you hadn’t told me this top cost less than $50, I would never have been able to tell,” said one Idaho tester after a ski tour on Teton Pass. “It’s comfy, breathable, and just kind of disappears.”

Both the tights and crew top are fitted but not overly tight for a sleek, next-to-skin fit that we put on and never fussed with. We found that the women’s base layer top wasn’t the warmest: On an 18-degree F day at Grand Targhee Resort, we found ourselves reaching for an extra midlayer. But on the plus side, we’ll keep these in the rotation for spring camping trips, too. The tights are closer to midweight, and we found them perfectly toasty for days ranging from 15 to 40 degrees F.

Pros and Cons + Extremely warm + Stylish – Not very breathable – Stretches out after wear

Cozy warmth meets Scandinavian style with the Kari Traa Saga Half Zip and Pants. Testers found this knit wool set to be the warmest in the women’s base layers test, providing sweet relief from the most frigid days on the hill. “This kit was perfect for early Tram at JHMR on a 10-degree F morning,” said one tester. “While everyone else was shivering, I was on the porch eating a waffle.”

The form-fitting design on both the top and bottom made layering easy, with thumb loops that slid easily into jackets. Testers loved the extra-wide waistband that provided support and fit smoothly underneath ski pant buckles (though we noticed some stretch after each wear, and opted to wash and dry these layers more frequently to maintain their shape over time). Throw a vest over the patterned design, and you’ve got a killer après outfit. While warmth earned high marks, versatility was a bit lower. We found the Saga kit to be a hit during December and January, but on sunny days in late February and March, we reached for lighter layers.

Sizes: XS-XL Key materials: Merino wool, polypropylene

Pros and Cons + Two-layer construction ideal for wool-sensitive people + Excellent moisture management + Flat seams are chafe-free under other layers – No thumb holes

Helly Hansen’s two-layer LIFA merino tech, which sandwiches an internal synthetic layer with an external merino layer, allows folks who are wool-sensitive to experience the material’s benefits. The result was a hit among our testers, who uniformly felt that LIFA’s combination of warmth and breathability made this the ultimate women’s base layer for any winter activity where big temperature swings make staying warm and dry essential.

While testing this set in regions ranging from the Northeast to the Tetons, testers agreed that the LIFA Merino Midweight crew top and bottom provided warmth on lift rides and windy summits but also wicked moisture extremely efficiently. One tester who happened to have it on while digging out her snowed-in van was impressed with how dry she stayed throughout the physical exertion. For folks wanting even more ventilation, HH makes a zip-neck version for quickly dumping heat.

Warm, breathable, quick drying, this is the lightweight zip-t you’ll be reaching for all season. This fleece pullover is perfect for cool mornings and versatile enough to act as a mid-layer in colder conditions. It’s also durable and water resistance to keep you protected from the elements and dry all day long. Layer it with anything, wear it in any conditions, and never miss a beat on the trail.

Pros and Cons + Lightweight + Hits at boot-top + Excellent movability – Runs a little short

A tried-and-true three-quarter option, Ortovox’s 100 percent merino Rock’N’Wool bottom gives you the barely-there feel you want during a hard-charging tour or in-bounds hike. They were the go-to women’s base layer for one tester who wore them all season, storm-skiing to spring touring, and appreciated the breathability, loose fit, and not having to maneuver ski socks around them. These are a lighter-weight bottom, and some testers who run cold found that they preferred a burlier base layer on the coldest days but picked these out of the pile on high-exertion outings and in temps of about 32 degrees F and above. It’s worth noting that if you have long legs, the bottom cuffs might ride up a bit.

Sizes: XS-L Key materials: Bamboo, merino wool, nylon

Pros and Cons + Extremely versatile + Hood adds protection – No XL size

Ski touring can feel like a temperature rollercoaster, and a lightweight base layer is typically the safest option. Le Bent’s Featherweight Hooded LS Crew has been available for a few seasons now, and no matter how many layers we try, we find ourselves returning to this women’s top time and time again. Testers took this lightweight, stretchy, stink-resistant top to Chamonix, British Columbia, and Hakuba, Japan.

Built with a blend of bamboo, merino, and nylon, this hoodie draws the best of everything from each fiber, with a smooth and luxurious next-to-skin feel. “I typically run cold, but get super hot with big efforts,” said one tester after a trip to Canada’s Selkirk Mountains. “This was my primary layer for a weeklong backcountry lodge trip in BC. A typical day was eight hours and 5,000 feet with lots of stopping, so temp control was key.”

We used the hood plenty while touring along windy ridges, and the relaxed fit was comfy to lounge in for lodge life. Our only complaint was the size run—most base layers for women we tested offered sizes up to XL, though this layer only goes up to a large.

Sizes: XS-XL Key materials: Merino wool, Tencel, nylon, spandex

Pros and Cons + Transitions between technical and casual + Super warm – Too warm for ski touring

Whether we were chasing early ups on a storm day or lounging around the house on a rest day, Stio’s Alpin PeakWool Midweight Hoodie got plenty of mileage. Built from a blend of merino, Tencel, nylon, and spandex, this women’s base layer struck a luxurious balance of warmth, stretchiness, and breathability. “I’d recommend this hoodie for anyone who skis midwinter,” said one tester after a season in the Sierra. “It’s comfortable and warm, and the combination of wool and smooth weave lets you forget you’re wearing technical clothing.” Though it felt a little too warm for a ski tour, we loved the cozy, relaxed fit for kicking back at the lodge after a big day on the hill.

Pros and Cons + Wide size run + Super breathable – Fair amount of stretch after wears

For yurt life, ski tours, and chasing our pals around the hill, we loved the cozy feel and breathability of the Wild Rye Bassett Half-Zip and Leggings. “My winter uniform,” reported one tester in Jackson, Wyoming, after a season of backcountry and resort skiing in the Tetons. Merino wool with a touch of spandex offered all the warmth, breathability, and odor resistance we could want out of a winter layer. The only downside was that we noticed a fair amount of stretch after each wear, and we found ourselves re-shrinking them in the dryer frequently.

Sizes: XS-XXL Key materials: Royal Alpaca fibers, merino, Tencel

Pros and Cons + Warm but not heavy + Odor-resistant + Form-fitting – Expensive

When it comes to base layers for women made from natural fibers, merino is by far the most readily available. But times are changing: bamboo has made an appearance over the last few years, and now alpaca is making its way onto the scene.

PAKA’s base layers are made with 40 percent Royal Alpaca fibers combined with 40 percent merino and 20 percent Tencel for stretch and comfort. Testers expected the Thermal Crew to be a little coarse, so they were pleasantly surprised to find otherwise. “This alpaca is super soft and very cozy,” commented a Jackson, Wyoming–based tester who wore it throughout the season in mild to moderate conditions. “I’ve tried others that are scratchy and itchy but this feels like a nice sweater.”

In addition to the top’s natural odor resistance, testers appreciated how the ribbed construction allowed it to hug curves without being too tight or restrictive. Consider PAKA if you’re wool-sensitive, but still want a warm and comfortable natural fiber top for pretty much any winter pursuit.

Sizes: XS-XL Key materials: Polyester, Lycra

Pros and Cons + Fun pattern + Highly breathable – Only for super warm days

A fun patterned base layer for passionate spring skiers, Skida’s Viska UPF Sun Hoodie became a solid corn-harvesting companion during April and May, when we just couldn’t bear to hang up the ski boots. “Why not add flowers to every ski day?” said one tester after a slushy day at Crystal Mountain in Washington. Made of polyester and Lycra, this women’s base layer is all about breathability, ideal for those 50-degree F days when you might ski in a bikini but know you need that sun protection.

When shopping for women’s base layers, consider how they will perform when you’re out in the wild. First and foremost, are they comfortable? These are your next-to-skin layers so they need to feel good on your body. Are the seams compatible with backpack straps? Does the hem work well with your ski pants? Does the half-zip, button-up, mock neck, or crew neck satisfy your style? Are you looking for something to wear during long days of uphill travel in the backcountry, or will you be spending the day riding chair lifts?

Merino wool fibers are naturally odor-resistant, moisture-wicking, and breathable, but generally less durable as synthetic materials. Because synthetics are usually a blend of fabrics, they are popular with those keen on stretchiness and moisture management. It’s better to buy something that is thoughtfully designed and durable rather than something cheaply made that might fail you on the mountain. Beyond the technical details, you may also want to determine if what you are buying is sustainably produced. Most reputable brands have information about their sustainability practices available on their websites.

Category manager Lily Krass Ritter lives in the Tetons, where she skis mostly at Jackson Hole Mountain Resort and tours on Teton Pass and in Grand Teton National Park. She recruited female testers all over the country, from age 20 through 63, who get after it year-round no matter the conditions. We had a dozen testers swapping women’s base layers in the Tetons, as well as other testers including ski patrollers at Crystal Mountain, Washington; ski instructors in the Berkshires; dedicated winter runners; and skiers in the Tahoe and Mammoth Lakes regions of California.

SLOMW’s Jen Affleck Calls Out Zac Affleck’s Behavior in Unseen Videos

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Secret Lives of Mormon Wives’ Jen Affleck shared never-before-seen videos with Zac Affleck’s behavior on display during their marriage in the hours after he addressed their divorce.

In the wake of news that the Secret Lives of Mormon Wives star filed for divorce from her husband Zac Affleck after seven years of marriage—and after he shared a five-minute video responding to their split—Jen is sharing a look at past moments from their relationship.

In the first never-before-seen video, Jen wrote over the clip, “POV: your [sic] two weeks postpartum and trying to become an influencer.”

While standing in a kitchen in her bathrobe, an off-camera Zac tells Jen, “I hope you’re not making a TikTok looking like that. Your boobs are literally—your tits are sagging all over.”

As Jen laughs in response, he adds, “You don’t want to post that, baby, I’ll tell you that right now.”

She then asks her husband, “Am I embarrassing myself?” To which Zac responds, “A little bit.”

Jen—who shares three kids with Zac and is currently pregnant with their fourth baby—captioned the TikTok, “He was never my biggest supporter.”

In a second video, filmed in their bathroom, Zac asks Jen why she left a towel on the ground, and she notes it was dirty.

“Well, what am I supposed to shower with?” he asks his wife. “Is there no other towels?”

As he walks out of the bathroom, she responds, “I’ve been doing all your laundry for the past—forever. If I got a little bit more help, maybe it’d be nice. Maybe we’d have some towels.”

As she chuckles into the mirror, Zac glares at her from the doorway before moving out of frame.

The latest videos from Jen were posted shortly after Zac shared a video addressing his and Jen’s split. In it, he alleges that while she first brought up parting ways a year ago, and he’d encouraged her to give their marriage another shot, he learned of her divorce filing via TMZ.

"I don't want anyone speaking bad about Jen," he said in the video. "I'll be the first to say I've made plenty of mistakes in our marriage, and it just really was time for us to go our own ways. We got married so young, and it sucks because I really did feel like we had something pretty special when we got married."

He added, “When I first met Jen, she had this light about her, unlike anyone I've ever met. And she really does have such a good heart, and I will continue to support her and be her biggest fan.”

Within the comments of his TikTok, one user referenced Jen’s videos, calling Zac out for calling Jen’s boobs “saggy” postpartum.

“This was a video from 2022. Obviously horrible and should have never been said,” he replied. “When I saw the video today I was absolutely shocked I had said that. I said a lot of horrible and hurtful things but I have tried my best to learn and grow.”

When another fan referenced the same moment, he added, “I think that world already knows I made a lot of mistakes and said a lot of absolutely horrible things.”

Nevertheless, he hopes he and Jen can find their footing as coparents, especially as they prepare to welcome their fourth baby.

“I feel like probably the thing that's eating me alive the most is I feel like I failed my kids, and I'm worried that I'm going to cause them damage,” he said in his video. “I just hope that we can still give them a normal childhood, but as far as that goes, Jen and I are definitely on the same page of wanting to give them the best the best life we can."

For a closer look at where the couples from the Secret Lives of Mormon Wives stand today, read on.

Taylor Frankie Paul & Dakota Mortensen: Broken Up

While Taylor Frankie Paul's romance with Dakota Mortensen—they welcomed son Ever in 2024—bloomed on The Secret Lives of Mormon Wives, they called it quits due to Dakota's infidelity.

"Right after Ever was born, Dakota and I were living together and in bliss with our new baby, and I was even considering engagement," Taylor—who began dating the real estate agent in July 2022—revealed during the season two premiere of the Hulu series. "And then I learned that he was lying this entire time."

Taylor was told by a woman named Jenna that she and Dakota had been intimate and he had "spent the night more than once." (He has denied the claims, writing in a September 2024 TikTok that he "never cheated.")

On the series, Taylor said that Dakota accused Jenna of instigating their affair.

"No, it was him," she emphasized in the confessional. "He said he had just kissed her on the couch. No, he went on the couch, they hooked up on the couch, and then they went to the room and they hooked up."

Since ending her romance with the Basin Tiling founder, the mom of three (she shares two kids with ex-husband Tate Paul) was set to find love once again on season 22 of The Bachelorette, but things got a bit thorny when news broke in March 2026 of an alleged confrontation between the exes weeks earlier.

A spokesperson for the Draper City Police Department confirmed to People that there was an open "domestic assault investigation," telling the outlet "allegations have been made in both directions.”

Days later, a video of a previous 2023 physical altercation between Taylor and Dakota surfaced online, prompting ABC to cancel the MomToker's season of the dating franchise.

As for Dakota, he has kicked off a romance with show guest star Shinia Powell, though he previously apologized to Taylor for hooking up with her during a season four episode.

Whitney Leavitt & Connor Leavitt: Married

Whitney Leavitt found the perfect dance partner in Connor Leavitt, whom she wed in 2016. But it wasn't always smooth sailing for the couple—who share kids Sedona, Liam and Billy—as they navigated life in the spotlight.

For instance, the Dancing With the Stars alum, who announced during her Broadway run in May 2026 that she'd be leaving Mormon Wives, had to shut down rumors about her husband's sexuality.

"Do I need to put out a sex tape, or something? Like, trust me," she said on the Off the Vine podcast in October 2024. "We're good. Everything's fine."

"I think it's because when we were going through our personal stuff—and I know you haven't seen it—but he struggled through a porn addiction," she explained. "There's a scene where one of my friends says something along the lines of how they could see how he was confused sexually."

Through the ups and downs, the reality star emphasized that she and Connor—who were 23 and 22, respectively, when they got married—are stronger than ever. In fact, they even renewed their vows in February 2026 for their 10th wedding anniversary.

"We got married really young," Conner told Brides after their ceremony. "It felt like our wedding day, as much as it was for us, it really felt like it was for our families. So we really want to do something to make it ours, and this felt like a really fun way to do it."

Married in 2019, Jen Affleck and Zac Affleck—no relation to Ben Affleck, despite jokes to the contrary—have been at the center of breakup rumors since SLOMW's debut.

In fact, their fight after Jen attended a Chippendales striptease show in Las Vegas became a focal point of the first season. The couple—who share kids Nora, Penelope and Lucas—made up, however, and the Dancing With the Stars contestant later defended their relationship.

I think it is frustrating for him when people don't see what's happening behind closed doors and how much work and effort he's putting in."

Jessi Draper & Jordan Ngatikaura: Divorced

Bumps in Jessi Draper and Jordan Ngatikaura's five-year marriage emerged after the hairstylist admitted to having an emotional affair with Vanderpump Villa's Marciano Brunette, though initially it appeared they were working towards smoother roads.

"We are back together," Jessi—who shares kids Jagger and Jovi with Jordan—told E! News in November 2025. "We're trying to work on it. Relationships are not perfect. I think a situation like ours—it's going to take time. It's not going to something that's just amazing overnight."

Sadly, the reconciliation appeared to be short-lived, with Jordan also dad to daughter Peyton from a previous relationship, filing for divorce in March 2026 after five years of marriage.

However, days later, Jessi said she was “blindsided” by his filing and accused him of rushing to submit the paperwork because “he wanted the headline first.”

Admitting that her “heart sunk” seeing the news, she explained, “because we agreed to tell our children together before filing and we haven’t done that.” (E! reached out to attorneys for Jordan for comment but haven’t heard back.)

Though Jessi told E! News that she was "just casually dating and living life," when she brought Marciano as her date to the 2026 American Music Awards in May 2026, he revealed a tattoo dedicated to her that August.

Jessi and Jordan finalized their divorce in September 2026.

Mayci didn't expect to find love with Jacob Neeley. In fact, after the unexpected death of her boyfriend Arik Mack in 2015, when she was just 14 weeks pregnant with their son Hudson, she was prepared to be a single mom.

But it all changed when she met Jacob in 2016 at age 21, whom she would marry two years later.

While Mikayla and Jace have been loving life with their four kids, Mikayla admitted that she hopes to grow their family even further.

"When people ask me how many kids I want, I'm like, 'I've always wanted three or four, but I could see myself having six,'" she told People in February 2025. "I'm excited to hopefully move into a new house. I want a big lot for my herd of kids."

"I think it's hard anytime you bring up the stuff with the sex or the intimacy," she admitted to Jace. "I feel like it's hard on me 'cause I feel like, again, my nervous system's already in fight or flight and I'm already maxed out. My bucket is full."

Morning Minute: Bitcoin Erases Election Rally as AI Stocks Crater

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

Morning Minute: Bitcoin Erases Election Rally as AI Stocks Crater

Morning Minute is a daily newsletter written by Tyler Warner. The analysis and opinions expressed are his own and do not necessarily reflect those of Decrypt. And check out our daily news show 'FOMO HOUR' covering all of the top stories and market action.

📉 Bitcoin Erases Election Rally as AI Stocks Crater

On Wednesday, the market decided a Democratic Congress was good news. Stocks had their best day in three months, Bitcoin climbed back over $80,800, and Trump posted on Truth Social that the market “just hit another Record High” with 401(k)s “through the roof.”

On Thursday, the market changed its mind.

The S&P 500 fell 4.36%, its worst session since the Liberation Day tariffs in April 2025. The Nasdaq dropped 5.52% and closed in correction, more than 10% below its record from last month. The Dow shed 1,833 points.

The damage concentrated in one place. The iShares Semiconductor ETF fell more than 12%, its worst day since March 2020. Micron lost 17.5%, AMD 17.3%, Marvell 16.4%, Nvidia 9.3%. The Magnificent Seven shed roughly $1 trillion in market value. The VIX touched 28.09 intraday, its highest since March. By the close, the U.S. stock market had lost $3T in value - more than the entire market cap of crypto.

Notably, the thing that spooked investors was the same election result that lifted everything a day earlier. Several states where Democrats won have active local opposition to data center construction, and the market spent Thursday pricing in slower permitting and tighter oversight of the AI buildout. Ipek Ozkardeskaya at Swissquote put it simply: “We suspect there is going to be some scrutiny on this aggressive AI buildout.” Rate expectations moved too, with December hike odds falling to 70.1% from 82.7%.

Crypto did not sit this one out. Bitcoin fell 5.4% to $75,871, below where it traded before the election. Ethereum dropped 7.6% to $2,239, a steeper fall than the Nasdaq. XRP lost 9.8%, and Hyperliquid fell 4.9%. The CMC20 index fell 6.29%. Almost $1.1B in crypto longs were liquidated on the day, before a slight relief rally overnight.

Two assets held up. Zcash fell 0.8% and Monero 2.2%, both less than the broad market, and both remain up on the week. Over the past year, Zcash is up 146% and Monero 74% while BTC is down 26% and ETH down 34%. The privacy trade is clearly still alive and looking as strong as ever.

Friday’s October jobs report is next. After a week where the market read the same political outcome two opposite ways inside two sessions, it is fair to wonder how much anyone actually knows right now. We will find out soon…

Best Sci-Fi Shows to Watch Before Apple TV’s ‘Neuromancer,’ Ranked

📅 2026-11-06  |  Read Source →

There's still nearly three months to go before Neuromancer premieres on Apple TV, but science fiction fans have plenty of other shows to hold them over in the meantime. Sci-fi has been flourishing on the small screen in the last decade or two, and chances are even the most die-hard fan has missed something great recently. The streaming era has been great for shows that need more special effects and bigger budgets, not to mention the welcome trend of adapting great books into series rather than movies. For those impatient to see Case (Callum Turner) and Molly (Briana Middleton) set out on their heist, read on for five sci-fi series you can watch now that are great fits for Neuromancer fans.

Neuromancer by William Gibson is a 1984 novel credited with popularizing the cyberpunk subgenre. In it, Gibson established many of the genre's most enduring tropes and concepts, even coining terms like "cyberspace" in his writing. The book is the first domino that led to later works like The Matrix and Altered Carbon, to name a few. However, this list looks beyond cyberpunk to shows with the same kinds of themes as Neuromancer. These are sci-fi series that don't shy away from the ugly truths, but don't dwell on them either.

While Foundation is in a completely different subgenre of sci-fi than Neuromancer, there are a lot of points of comparison between the two. Foundation is adapted from Isaac Asimov's novel series by the same name, which is just as important to space opera sci-fi as Neuromancer is to cyberpunk. It's one of Apple's biggest and most ambitious productions, and it may give us some important hints about where the streamer is taking Neuromancer. Showrunners David S. Goyer and Josh Friedman have not been shy about making additions or changes to the source material, and it has worked out well for them. We may see similar strategies here.

Foundation takes place in a distant future where humanity has colonized much of the galaxy and forged it into a massive empire. The empire is so vast that it's not even clear to everyone when it begins fracturing, but one prescient scientist develops a means of seeing ahead. He and his students seek to hold off the worst of the entropy and minimize the dark age between the fall of this empire and the rise of something new. The result is a story that spans planets, centuries, and even millennia.

The genre-bending 3 Body Problem is a great series to pick up in these final weeks of waiting for Neuromancer. The show is adapted from a trilogy of sci-fi novels by Liu Cixin called Remembrance of Earth's Past, with the first volume titled The Three-Body Problem. The books were originally published in Mandarin, with English translations by Ken Liu published from 2014 to 2016. The series is about earth's first contact with an alien civilization, and it's a brutal examination of how and why intergalactic connections might form.

The adaptation kicked off in 2024 on Netflix, and Season 2 was expected to premiere sometime in 2026. With no announcement so far the series may be delayed, but Season 2 has already been filmed and it has even been rumored that Season 3 was filmed alongside it, so more episodes are on the way. This is a perfect leapfrog situation for new viewers — you can dive into 3 Body Problem while waiting for Neuromancer, then by the time Neuromancer is over you may have new episodes of 3 Body Problem to enjoy.

Apple TV's Silo might have more in common with Neuromancer — at least in terms of genre and setting. The show takes place in a dystopian future where humanity seems to be confined to one underground shelter known as the silo. However, there are conspicuous mysteries about how they ended up there and what the world outside really looks like. The show masterfully teases out these mysteries, and I won't spoil anything good here. Suffice it say, this is a must-watch for fans of conspiracies, survival stories, and near-future sci-fi.

"My name is Kathy H. I'm 31 years old, and I've been a carer now for over 11 years."

Silo doesn't have the same cyberpunk flavor as Neuromancer and other stories in the genre, but it doesn't confine itself from those things either. The TV adaptation in particular does some fun things with technology and set design that will appeal to fans of cyberpunk. It also fleshes out its characters into the kinds of underdog heroes we all love to root for in these stories.

Cyberpunk is best known for its bold style, and no series brought as much cool-factor to the genre as Cowboy Bebop. The live action adaptation is available now on Netflix, while the original anime is currently streaming on Crunchyroll. Both are the perfect binge-watch before Neuromancer comes out. The series is a space western following bounty hunters on adventures throughout the solar system. The show has something for everyone — especially those who are already looking forward to Neuromancer. The anime is only 26 episodes long, so even if you've seen it before, a rewatch might be in order as we wait for something new.

Finally, of all the great sci-fi shows in the last two decades, nothing has evoked Neuromancer quite like HBO's Westworld. The show reimagined the dystopian theme park setting from the original 1973 film, filling the screen with action while tackling existential questions about the nature of consciousness and humanity. Westworld is a must-watch show today as it criticizes big tech in a way that seems more relevant with each passing year. Sadly, the series was removed from HBO Max a few years ago, and is only available today on Blu-ray. Still, it's worth seeking out.

There are plenty of other great sci-fi stories to check out as we wait for Apple TV's Neuromancer to bring the original novel to life. The show premieres on January 22 on Apple TV. In the meantime, Gibson's novels are currently available in print, digital, and audiobook formats.
